\doxysubsection*{\+: The name of the trace array to be looked up/created.}
\label{_amgrpcd3db8d01f4859dc61f9ebde1c59710e}%
trace\+\_\+array\+\_\+get\+\_\+by\+\_\+name -\/ Create/\+Lookup a trace array, given its name.

Returns pointer to trace array with given name. N\+U\+LL, if it cannot be created.

N\+O\+TE\+: This function increments the reference counter associated with the trace array returned. This makes sure it cannot be freed while in use. Use \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_ae2934950a9c7bd99e5ed10e599e9ee84}{trace\+\_\+array\+\_\+put()}} once the trace array is no longer needed. If the \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__array}{trace\+\_\+array}} is to be freed, \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_a5949120823f3fc9ab1e24de7bdcbf345}{trace\+\_\+array\+\_\+destroy()}} needs to be called after the \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_ae2934950a9c7bd99e5ed10e599e9ee84}{trace\+\_\+array\+\_\+put()}}, or simply let user space delete it from the tracefs instances directory. But until the \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_ae2934950a9c7bd99e5ed10e599e9ee84}{trace\+\_\+array\+\_\+put()}} is called, user space can not delete it. \begin{DoxyCompactItemize}
\item

trace\+\_\+array\+\_\+printk -\/ Print a message to a specific instance @tr\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} instance \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__array}{trace\+\_\+array}} descriptor @ip\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} instruction pointer that this is called from. @fmt\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} format to print (printf format)

If a subsystem sets up its own instance, they have the right to printk strings into their tracing instance buffer using this function. Note, this function will not write into the top level buffer (use trace\+\_\+printk() for that), as writing into the top level buffer should only have events that can be individually disabled. trace\+\_\+printk() is only used for debugging a kernel, and should not be ever incorporated in normal use.

trace\+\_\+array\+\_\+printk() can be used, as it will not add noise to the top level tracing buffer.

Note, \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_a260dd730915e5458936d951fb1425768}{trace\+\_\+array\+\_\+init\+\_\+printk()}} must be called on @tr before this can be used. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

err\+\_\+pos -\/ find the position of a string within a command for error careting @cmd\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} tracing command that caused the error @str\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} string to position the caret at within @cmd

Finds the position of the first occurrence of @str within @cmd. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} return value can be passed to \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_a00454c6a4e1af22dc8f3ad466198ea3b}{tracing\+\_\+log\+\_\+err()}} for caret placement within @cmd.

Returns the index within @cmd of the first occurrence of @str or 0 if @str was not found. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

trace\+\_\+buffered\+\_\+event\+\_\+disable -\/ disable buffering events

When a filter is removed, it is faster to not use the buffered events, and to commit directly into the ring buffer. Free up the temp buffers when there are no more users. This requires special synchronization with current events. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 221
Here is the caller graph for this function\+:
% FIG 222
\mbox{\Hypertarget{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_a27ef435a51e44a602740f4118e6db40a}\label{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_a27ef435a51e44a602740f4118e6db40a}} 
\index{trace.c@{trace.c}!trace\_buffered\_event\_enable@{trace\_buffered\_event\_enable}}
\index{trace\_buffered\_event\_enable@{trace\_buffered\_event\_enable}!trace.c@{trace.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{trace\_buffered\_event\_enable()}{trace\_buffered\_event\_enable()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void trace\+\_\+buffered\+\_\+event\+\_\+enable (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{ }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

trace\+\_\+buffered\+\_\+event\+\_\+enable -\/ enable buffering events

When events are being filtered, it is quicker to use a temporary buffer to write the event data into if there\textquotesingle{}s a likely chance that it will not be committed. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} discard of the ring buffer is not as fast as committing, and is much slower than copying a commit.

When an event is to be filtered, allocate per cpu buffers to write the event data into, and if the event is filtered and discarded it is simply dropped, otherwise, the entire data is to be committed in one shot. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 223
Here is the caller graph for this function\+:
% FIG 224
\mbox{\Hypertarget{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_aa44c63cebe3fe030628909dd4c90cac0}\label{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_aa44c63cebe3fe030628909dd4c90cac0}}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{trace\_check\_vprintf()}{trace\_check\_vprintf()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void trace\+\_\+check\+\_\+vprintf (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__iterator}{trace\+\_\+iterator}} $\ast$}]{iter,  }\item[{const char $\ast$}]{fmt,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{stdarg_8h_a2ed76bdaf83448883f275ac530e804e9}{va\+\_\+list}}}]{ap }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

trace\+\_\+check\+\_\+vprintf -\/ Check dereferenced strings while writing to the seq buffer @iter\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} iterator that holds the seq buffer and the event being printed @fmt\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} format used to print the event @ap\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} va\+\_\+list holding the data to print from @fmt.

This writes the data into the @iter-\/$>$seq buffer using the data from @fmt and @ap. If the format has a s, then the source of the string is examined to make sure it is safe to print, otherwise it will warn and print \char`\"{}\mbox{[}\+U\+N\+S\+A\+F\+E M\+E\+M\+O\+R\+Y\mbox{]}\char`\"{} in place of the dereferenced string pointer. \doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{trace\_filter\_add\_remove\_task()}{trace\_filter\_add\_remove\_task()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void trace\+\_\+filter\+\_\+add\+\_\+remove\+\_\+task (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__pid__list}{trace\+\_\+pid\+\_\+list}} $\ast$}]{pid\+\_\+list,  }\item[{struct task\+\_\+struct $\ast$}]{self,  }\item[{struct task\+\_\+struct $\ast$}]{task }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

trace\+\_\+filter\+\_\+add\+\_\+remove\+\_\+task -\/ Add or remove a task from a pid\+\_\+list @pid\+\_\+list\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} list to modify @self\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} current task for fork or N\+U\+LL for exit @task\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} task to add or remove

If adding a task, if @self is defined, the task is only added if @self is also included in @pid\+\_\+list. This happens on fork and tasks should only be added when the parent is listed. If @self is N\+U\+LL, then the @task pid will be removed from the list, which would happen on exit of a task. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

tracing\+\_\+is\+\_\+enabled -\/ Show if global\+\_\+trace has been enabled

Shows if the global trace has been enabled or not. It uses the mirror flag \char`\"{}buffer\+\_\+disabled\char`\"{} to be used in fast paths such as for the irqsoff tracer. But it may be inaccurate due to races. If you need to know the accurate state, use \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_ab66b1dd4be2fc3fa5b3678f173599349}{tracing\+\_\+is\+\_\+on()}} which is a little slower, but accurate. {\footnotesize\ttfamily void \mbox{\hyperlink{structtracing__log__err}{tracing\+\_\+log\+\_\+err}} (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structtrace__array}{trace\+\_\+array}} $\ast$}]{tr,  }\item[{const char $\ast$}]{loc,  }\item[{const char $\ast$}]{cmd,  }\item[{const char $\ast$$\ast$}]{errs,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{build_8c_aed742c436da53c1080638ce6ef7d13de}{u8}}}]{type,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{build_8c_aed742c436da53c1080638ce6ef7d13de}{u8}}}]{pos }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\mbox{\hyperlink{structtracing__log__err}{tracing\+\_\+log\+\_\+err}} -\/ write an error to the tracing error log @tr\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} associated trace array for the error (N\+U\+LL for top level array) @loc\+: A string describing where the error occurred @cmd\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} tracing command that caused the error @errs\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} array of loc-\/specific static error strings @type\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} index into errs\mbox{[}\mbox{]}, which produces the specific static err string @pos\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} position the caret should be placed in the cmd

Writes an error into tracing/error\+\_\+log of the form\+:

$<$loc$>$\+: error\+: $<$text$>$ Command\+: $<$cmd$>$ $^\wedge$

tracing/error\+\_\+log is a small log file containing the last T\+R\+A\+C\+I\+N\+G\+\_\+\+L\+O\+G\+\_\+\+E\+R\+R\+S\+\_\+\+M\+AX errors (8). Memory for errors isn\textquotesingle{}t allocated unless there has been a tracing error, and the error log can be cleared and have its memory freed by writing the empty string in truncation mode to it i.\+e. echo $>$ tracing/error\+\_\+log.

N\+O\+TE\+: the @errs array along with the @type param are used to produce a static error string -\/ this string is not copied and saved when the error is logged -\/ only a pointer to it is saved. See existing callers for examples of how static strings are typically defined for use with \mbox{\hyperlink{kernel_2trace_2trace_8c_a00454c6a4e1af22dc8f3ad466198ea3b}{tracing\+\_\+log\+\_\+err()}}. tracing\+\_\+off -\/ turn off tracing buffers

This function stops the tracing buffers from recording data. It does not disable any overhead the tracers themselves may be causing. This function simply causes all recording to the ring buffers to fail. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

tracing\+\_\+update\+\_\+buffers -\/ used by tracing facility to expand ring buffers

To save on memory when the tracing is never used on a system with it configured in. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} ring buffers are set to a minimum size. But once a user starts to use the tracing facility, then they need to grow to their default size.

This function is to be called when a tracer is about to be used. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
